<div align="left" data-conditional="_fmc_v2_fmc_sram_nand,_fmc_v2_fsmc_sram_nand,_fmc_v2_sram_nand_sdram"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.11px"/><col width="500.06px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FEMPT: </span>FIFO empty</p><p class="TableIndent">Read-only bit that provides the status of the FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FIFO not empty<br/>1: FIFO empty</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IFEN: </span>Interrupt falling edge detection enable bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt falling edge detection request disabled<br/>1: Interrupt falling edge detection request enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ILEN: </span>Interrupt high-level detection enable bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt high-level detection request disabled<br/>1: Interrupt high-level detection request enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IREN: </span>Interrupt rising edge detection enable bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt rising edge detection request disabled<br/>1: Interrupt rising edge detection request enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IFS: </span>Interrupt falling edge status</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is set by hardware and reset by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No interrupt falling edge occurred<br/>1: Interrupt falling edge occurred</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If this bit is written by software to 1 it is set.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ILS: </span>Interrupt high-level status</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is set by hardware and reset by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Interrupt high-level occurred<br/>1: Interrupt high-level occurred</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IRS: </span>Interrupt rising edge status</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is set by hardware and reset by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No interrupt rising edge occurred<br/>1: Interrupt rising edge occurred</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If this bit is written by software to 1 it is set.</p></td></tr></table></div>


#### Common memory space timing register (FMC\_PMEM)

Address offset: 0x88

Reset value: 0xFCFC FCFC

The FMC\_PMEM read/write register contains the timing information for NAND flash memory bank. This information is used to access either the common memory space of the NAND flash for command, address write access and data read/write access.

<div align="left" data-conditional="_fmc_v2_fmc_sram_nand,_fmc_v2_fsmc_sram_nand,_fmc_v2_sram_nand_sdram"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.44px"/><col width="498.74px"/><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MEMHIZ[7:0]: </span>Common memory x data bus Hi-Z time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the number of HCLK clock cycles during which the data bus is kept Hi-Z after the start of a NAND flash write access to common memory space on socket. This is only valid for write <span class="Question" style="color:#000000;">transactions:</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: <span class="Question" style="color:#000000;">1 HCLK cycle </span></p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1110: <span class="Question" style="color:#000000;">255 HCLK cycles</span></p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="145.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MEMHOLD[7:0]: </span>Common memory hold time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the number of HCLK clock cycles <span style="font-kerning:none;">for write access and HCLK (+2) clock cycles for read access </span>during which the address is held (and data for write accesses) after the command is deasserted (NWE, NOE), for NAND flash read or write access to common memory space on socket x:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: reserved.<br/><span class="Question" style="color:#000000;">0000 0001: 1 HCLK cycle </span>for write access / 3 HCLK cycles for read access<br/>1111 1110: <span class="Question" style="color:#000000;">254 HCLK cycles </span>for write access / 256 HCLK cycles for read access</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="145.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MEMWAIT[7:0]: </span>Common memory wait time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the minimum number of HCLK (+1) clock cycles to assert the command (NWE, NOE), for NAND flash read or write access to common memory space on socket. The duration of command assertion is extended if the wait signal (NWAIT) is active (low) at the end of the programmed value of HCLK:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: reserved<br/>0000 0001: 2HCLK cycles (+ wait cycle introduced by deasserting NWAIT)</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1110: 255 HCLK cycles (+ wait cycle introduced by deasserting NWAIT)<br/>1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MEMSET[7:0]: </span>Common memory x setup time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the number of HCLK (+1) clock cycles to set up the address before the command assertion (NWE, NOE), for NAND flash read or write access to common memory space on socket x:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: 1 HCLK cycle<br/>1111 1110: 255 HCLK cycles</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1111: reserved</p></td></tr></table></div>


#### Attribute memory space timing register (FMC\_PATT)

Address offset: 0x8C

Reset value: 0xFCFC FCFC

The FMC\_PATT read/write register contains the timing information for NAND flash memory bank. It is used for 8-bit accesses to the attribute memory space of the NAND flash for the last address write access if the timing must differ from that of previous accesses (for Ready/Busy management, refer to [Section 19.7.5: NAND flash prewait functionality](#XREF_36352__42_6_5_NAND_Flash_prewait_functionality6)).

<div align="left" data-conditional="_fmc_v2_fmc_sram_nand,_fmc_v2_fsmc_sram_nand,_fmc_v2_sram_nand_sdram"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="114.53px"/><col width="499.64px"/><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ATTHIZ[7:0]: </span>Attribute memory data bus Hi-Z time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the number of HCLK clock cycles during which the data bus is kept in Hi-Z after the start of a NAND flash write access to attribute memory space on socket. Only valid for writ transaction:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: 1 HCLK cycle</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1110: 255 HCLK cycles</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="148.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ATTHOLD[7:0]: </span>Attribute memory hold time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the number of HCLK clock cycles <span style="font-kerning:none;">for write access and HCLK (+2) clock cycles for read access </span>during which the address is held (and data for write access) after the command deassertion (NWE, NOE), for NAND flash read or write access to attribute memory space on socket:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0001: 1 HCLK cycle for write access / 3 HCLK cycles for read access</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1110: 254 HCLK cycles for write access / 256 HCLK cycles for read access</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="146.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ATTWAIT[7:0]: </span>Attribute memory wait time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the minimum number of HCLK (+1) clock cycles to assert the command (NWE, NOE), for NAND flash read or write access to attribute memory space on socket x. The duration for command assertion is extended if the wait signal (NWAIT) is active (low) at the end of the programmed value of HCLK:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0001: 2 HCLK cycles (+ wait cycle introduced by deassertion of NWAIT)</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1110: 255 HCLK cycles (+ wait cycle introduced by deasserting NWAIT)<br/>1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ATTSET[7:0]: </span>Attribute memory setup time</p><p class="TableIndent">Defines the number of HCLK (+1) clock cycles to set up address before the command assertion (NWE, NOE), for NAND flash read or write access to attribute memory space on socket:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000 0000: 1 HCLK cycle</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1110: 255 HCLK cycles</p><p class="TableIndent">1111 1111: reserved.</p></td></tr></table></div>


#### ECC result registers (FMC\_ECCR)

Address offset: 0x94

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

This register contain the current error correction code value computed by the ECC computation modules of the FMC NAND controller. When the CPU reads the data from a NAND flash memory page at the correct address (refer to [Section 19.7.6: Computation of the error correction code (ECC) in NAND flash memory](#XREF_59805__42_6_6_Computation_of_the_error_correction_code_(ECC)_i6)), the data read/written from/to the NAND flash memory are processed automatically by the ECC computation module. When X bytes have been read (according to the ECCPS field in the FMC\_PCR registers), the CPU must read the computed ECC value from the FMC\_ECC registers. It then verifies if these computed parity data are the same as the parity value recorded in the spare area, to determine whether a page is valid, and, to correct it otherwise. The FMC\_ECCR register must be cleared after being read by setting the ECCEN bit to 0. To compute a new data block, the ECCEN bit must be set to 1.
